STEP 32 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
path = 'pest/src/parser_state.rs'
src = open(path).read()

old = """    /// Attempts to skip `n` characters forward. Returns `Ok` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
    /// if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>` otherwise.
"""
new = """    /// Attempts to match a single character contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok` with
    /// the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
    /// otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
    ///
    /// # Examples
    ///
    /// ```
    /// # use pest;
    /// # #[allow(non_camel_case_types)]
    /// # #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
    /// enum Rule {}
    ///
    /// let input = "ab";
    /// let mut state: Box<pest::ParserState<'_, Rule>> = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// let mut result = state.match_char_class(&[('a', 'c')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_ok());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap().position().pos(), 1);
    ///
    /// state = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// result = state.match_char_class(&[('x', 'z')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_err());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().position().pos(), 0);
    /// ```
    #[inline]
    pub fn match_char_class(
        mut self: Box<Self>,
        ranges: &[(char, char)],
    ) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
        let start_position = self.position.pos();
        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
            ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
        });
        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to match a single character not contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok`
    /// with the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated
    /// `Box<ParserState>` otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
    ///
    /// # Examples
    ///
    /// ```
    /// # use pest;
    /// # #[allow(non_camel_case_types)]
    /// # #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
    /// enum Rule {}
    ///
    /// let input = "ab";
    /// let mut state: Box<pest::ParserState<'_, Rule>> = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// let mut result = state.match_neg_char_class(&[('b', 'z')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_ok());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap().position().pos(), 1);
    ///
    /// state = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// result = state.match_neg_char_class(&[('a', 'c')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_err());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().position().pos(), 0);
    /// ```
    #[inline]
    pub fn match_neg_char_class(
        mut self: Box<Self>,
        ranges: &[(char, char)],
    ) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
        let start_position = self.position.pos();
        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
            !ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
        });
        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to skip `n` characters forward. Returns `Ok` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
    /// if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>` otherwise.
"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)
open(path, 'w').write(src)
print("methods inserted")

    ranges
        .into_iter()

            let start = start.chars().next().expect("empty range start");
            let end = end.chars().next().expect("empty range end");
            quote! { (#start, #end) }
        })
        .collect()
}

cd /app && cat > vm/tests/coalescing.pest <<'EOF'
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Licensed under MIT/Apache-2.0

full_range = { 'a' | 'b' | 'c' }
two_ranges = { 'a' | 'b' | 'c' | 'x' | 'y' | 'z' }
partial = { 'a' | ident | 'b' | 'c' | 'd' }
ident = { "foo" }
negated = { !('a' | 'b' | 'c') ~ ANY }
negated_single = { !'x' ~ ANY }

cat > vm/tests/coalescing.rs <<'EOF'
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Licensed under MIT/Apache-2.0

extern crate pest;
extern crate pest_meta;
#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_vm;

use pest_meta::optimizer::{OptimizedExpr, OptimizedRule};
use pest_meta::parser::Rule;
use pest_meta::{optimizer, parser};
use pest_vm::Vm;

const GRAMMAR: &str = include_str!("coalescing.pest");

fn optimized() -> Vec<OptimizedRule> {
    let pairs = parser::parse(Rule::grammar_rules, GRAMMAR).unwrap();
    let ast = parser::consume_rules(pairs).unwrap();
    optimizer::optimize(ast)
}

#[test]
fn full_range_collapses() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "full_range").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::Range("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())
    );
}

#[test]
fn char_class_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "two_ranges").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::CharClass(vec![
            ("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned()),
            ("x".to_owned(), "z".to_owned()),
        ])
    );
}

#[test]
fn partial_run_is_coalesced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "partial").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::Choice(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Str("a".to_owned())),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Choice(
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ident".to_owned())),
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Range("b".to_owned(), "d".to_owned())),
            )),
        )
    );
}

#[test]
fn neg_char_class_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "negated").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())])
    );
}

#[test]
fn neg_char_class_single_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "negated_single").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("x".to_owned(), "x".to_owned())])
    );
}

#[test]
fn vm_parses_char_class() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let vm = Vm::new(rules);
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "c").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "d").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "b").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "y").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "m").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "c").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "foo").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "e").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("negated", "d").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated", "a").is_err());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated_single", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated_single", "x").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "A").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "d").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "e").is_err());
}
EOF

    56	    /// (used by the `pest_debugger` crate)
    57	    pub fn new_with_listener(rules: Vec<OptimizedRule>, listener: ListenerFn) -> Vm {

    59	        Vm {

    61	            listener: Some(listener),
    62	        }

    65	    /// Runs a parser rule on an input
    66	    #[allow(clippy::perf)]
    67	    pub fn parse<'a>(
    68	        &'a self,
    69	        rule: &'a str,
    70	        input: &'a str,
    71	    ) -> Result<Pairs<'a, &'a str>, Error<&'a str>> {
    72	        pest::state(input, |state| self.parse_rule(rule, state))
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    #[allow(clippy::suspicious)]
    76	    fn parse_rule<'a>(
    77	        &'a self,
    78	        rule: &'a str,
    79	        state: Box<ParserState<'a, &'a str>>,
    80	    ) -> ParseResult<Box<ParserState<'a, &'a str>>> {
    81	        if let Some(ref listener) = self.listener {
    82	            if listener(rule.to_owned(), state.position()) {
    83	                return Err(ParserState::new(state.position().line_of()));
    84	            }
    85	        }
    86	        match rule {
    87	            "ANY" => return state.skip(1),
    88	            "EOI" => return state.rule("EOI", |state| state.end_of_input()),
    89	            "SOI" => return state.start_of_input(),
    90	            "PEEK" => return state.stack_peek(),
    91	            "PEEK_ALL" => return state.stack_match_peek(),
    92	            "POP" => return state.stack_pop(),
    93	            "POP_ALL" => return state.stack_match_pop(),
    94	            "DROP" => return state.stack_drop(),
    95	            "ASCII_DIGIT" => return state.match_range('0'..'9'),

END STEP

returncode=0

/// A PEEK expression.
peek_slice = { "PEEK" ~ opening_brack ~ integer? ~ range_operator ~ integer? ~ closing_brack }

/// An identifier.
identifier = @{ !"PUSH" ~ ("_" | alpha) ~ ("_" | alpha_num)* }

/// An alpha character.
alpha = _{ 'a'..'z' | 'A'..'Z' }

/// An alphanumeric character.
alpha_num = _{ alpha | '0'..'9' }

/// A string.
string = ${ quote ~ inner_str ~ quote }

/// An insensitive string.
insensitive_string = { "^" ~ string }

/// A character range.
range = { character ~ range_operator ~ character }

/// A single quoted character
character = ${ single_quote ~ inner_chr ~ single_quote }

/// A quoted string.
inner_str = @{ (!("\"" | "\\") ~ ANY)* ~ (escape ~ inner_str)? }

/// An escaped or any character.
inner_chr = @{ escape | ANY }

/// An escape sequence.
escape = @{ "\\" ~ ("\"" | "\\" | "r" | "n" | "t" | "0" | "'" | code | unicode) }

/// A hexadecimal code.
code = @{ "x" ~ hex_digit{2} }

/// A unicode code.
unicode = @{ "u" ~ opening_brace ~ hex_digit{2, 6} ~ closing_brace }

/// A hexadecimal digit.

END STEP

    html_logo_url = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pest-parser/pest/master/pest-logo.svg",
    html_favicon_url = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pest-parser/pest/master/pest-logo.svg"
)]
#![warn(missing_docs, rust_2018_idioms, unused_qualifications)]

#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_derive;

pub use pest::Parser;

/// Grammar rules of a simplified HTTP request parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod http {
    /// HTTP parser.
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/http.pest"]
    pub struct HttpParser;
}

/// Grammar rules of a sample JSON parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod json {
    /// JSON parser.
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/json.pest"]
    pub struct JsonParser;
}

/// Grammar rules of a sample TOML parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod toml {
    /// TOML parser.
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/toml.pest"]
    pub struct TomlParser;
}

/// Grammar rules of an SQL parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod sql {
    /// SQL parser.
    /// Grammar is a tinkered version of the one used in distributed SQL executor module named
    /// [sbroad](https://git.picodata.io/picodata/picodata/sbroad/-/blob/main/sbroad-core/src/frontend/sql/query.pest).
    /// Being a submodule of [Picodata](https://git.picodata.io/picodata/picodata/picodata) (that
    /// operates with Tarantool database) it tries to simulate SQLite flavour (Tarantool uses
    /// SQLite to execute SQL queries).
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/sql.pest"]
    pub struct SqlParser;
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use pest::iterators::Pairs;
    use std::convert::TryInto;

    use pest::pratt_parser::PrattParser;
    use pest::Parser;

    use crate::{json, sql, toml};

    fn test_toml_deep_nesting(input: &str) {
        const ERROR: &str = "call limit reached";
        pest::set_call_limit(Some(5_000usize.try_into().unwrap()));
        let s = toml::TomlParser::parse(toml::Rule::toml, input);
        assert!(s.is_err());
        assert_eq!(s.unwrap_err().variant.message(), ERROR);
    }

    #[test]
    fn toml_handles_deep_nesting() {
        let sample1 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/tomlfuzzsample1.toml"
        ));
        test_toml_deep_nesting(sample1);
    }

    #[test]
    #[ignore = "this sometimes crashes in the debug mode"]
    fn toml_handles_deep_nesting_unstable() {
        let sample2 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/tomlfuzzsample2.toml"
        ));
        test_toml_deep_nesting(sample2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn json_handles_deep_nesting() {
        let sample1 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/jsonfuzzsample1.json"
        ));
        let sample2 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/jsonfuzzsample2.json"
        ));
        const ERROR: &str = "call limit reached";
        pest::set_call_limit(Some(5_000usize.try_into().unwrap()));
        let s1 = json::JsonParser::parse(json::Rule::json, sample1);
        assert!(s1.is_err());
        assert_eq!(s1.unwrap_err().variant.message(), ERROR);
        let s2 = json::JsonParser::parse(json::Rule::json, sample2);
        assert!(s2.is_err());
        assert_eq!(s2.unwrap_err().variant.message(), ERROR);
    }

    #[test]
    fn sql_check_expressions_priorities() {
        lazy_static::lazy_static! {
            static ref PRATT_PARSER: PrattParser<sql::Rule> = {
                use pest::pratt_parser::{Assoc::{Left, Right}, Op};
                use sql::Rule::{Add, And, Between, ConcatInfixOp, Divide, Eq, Gt, GtEq, In,
                                IsNullPostfix, Lt, LtEq, Multiply, NotEq, Or, Subtract, UnaryNot};

                // Precedence is defined lowest to highest.
                PrattParser::new()
                    .op(Op::infix(Or, Left))
                    .op(Op::infix(Between, Left))
                    .op(Op::infix(And, Left))
                    .op(Op::prefix(UnaryNot))
                    .op(
                        Op::infix(Eq, Right) | Op::infix(NotEq, Right) | Op::infix(NotEq, Right)
                            | Op::infix(Gt, Right) | Op::infix(GtEq, Right) | Op::infix(Lt, Right)
                            | Op::infix(LtEq, Right) | Op::infix(In, Right)
                    )
                    .op(Op::infix(Add, Left) | Op::in
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
 right: Box::new(Expr::ArithValue(2)),
                        }),
                    }),
                    op: InfixOp::BoolInfix(BoolOp::Eq),
                    right: Box::new(Expr::ArithValue(3)),
                }),
            }),
            right: Box::new(Expr::Infix {
                left: Box::new(Expr::IsNull {
                    child: Box::new(Expr::BoolConst(false)),
                }),
                op: InfixOp::BoolInfix(BoolOp::And),
                right: Box::new(Expr::Infix {
                    left: Box::new(Expr::ArithValue(1)),
                    op: InfixOp::BoolInfix(BoolOp::In),
                    right: Box::new(Expr::SubQuery),
                }),
            }),
        };
        assert_eq!(expected_expr, actual_expr);
    }

    #[test]
    fn sql_parse_attempts_error() {
        pest::set_error_detail(true);

        fn is_whitespace(string: String) -> bool {
            string == "\r\n"
                || (string.len() == 1 && string.chars().next().unwrap().is_whitespace())
        }

        fn rule_to_message(r: &sql::Rule) -> Option<String> {
            match r {
                sql::Rule::CreateTable => Some(String::from("Expected table creation.")),
                sql::Rule::PrimaryKey => Some(String::from(
                    "Add primary key consisting of non nullable table columns.",
                )),
                sql::Rule::CreateUser => Some(String::from("Expected user creation.")),
                sql::Rule::SingleQuotedString => {
                    Some(String::from("Add a string in single quotes."))
                }
                sql::Rule::Query => Some(String::from("DML query expected.")),
                sql::Rule::Expr => Some(String::from("Expected expression.")),
                _ => None,
            }
        }

        type RuleToMessageBoxed = Box<dyn Fn(&sql::Rule) -> Option<String>>;
        type IsWhiteSpaceBoxed = Box<dyn Fn(String) -> bool>;

        let rule_to_message_boxed: RuleToMessageBoxed = Box::new(rule_to_message);
        let is_whitespace_boxed: IsWhiteSpaceBoxed = Box::new(is_whitespace);

        let retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string = |input| {
            let e = sql::SqlParser::parse(sql::Rule::Command, input).unwrap_err();
            let parse_attempt_error = e
                .parse_attempts_error(input, &rule_to_message_boxed, &is_whitespace_boxed)
                .unwrap();
            format!("{parse_attempt_error}")
        };

        let table_creation_without_primary_key =
            r#"create table t(col_1 int,) distributed by (col_1)"#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(table_creation_without_primary_key),
            [
                " --> 1:26",
                "  |",
                "1 | create table t(col_1 int,) distributed by (col_1)",
                "  |                          ^---",
                "  |",
                "  = error: parsing error occurred.",
                r#"    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `"`, `-`, `A..Z`, `PRIMARY`, `_`, `a..z`, `А..Я`, `а..я`"#,
                "    help: Expected table creation.",
                "          - Add primary key consisting of non nullable table columns.",
            ]
            .join("\n")
        );

        let user_creation_password_without_single_quotes = r#"create user
                                                                   Bob password "wrong""#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(user_creation_password_without_single_quotes),
            [
                " --> 2:81",
                "  |",
                r#"2 |                                                                    Bob password "wrong""#,
                "  |                                                                                 ^---",
                "  |",
                "  = error: parsing error occurred.",
                "    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `''`, `'`",
                "    help: Expected user creation.",
                "          - Add a string in single quotes.",
            ]
                .join("\n")
        );

        let invalid_expression_in_projection = r#"select 1 + from t"#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(invalid_expression_in_projection),
            [
                " --> 1:12",
                "  |",
                "1 | select 1 + from t",
                "  |            ^---",
                "  |",
                "  = error: parsing error occurred.",
                r#"    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `"`, `$`, `''`, `'`, `(`, `+`, `-`, `0..9`, `?`, `CAST`, `EXISTS`, `FALSE`, `NOT`, `NULL`, `TRUE`"#,
                "    note: unexpected token: `FROM`",
                "    help: DML query expected.",
                "          - Expected expression.",
            ]
                .join("\n")
        );
    }
}

extern crate alloc;
use alloc::{format, vec::Vec};

#[macro_use]
extern crate pest;
#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_derive;

#[derive(Parser)]
#[grammar = "tests/grammar.pest"]
struct GrammarParser;

#[test]
fn string() {
    parses_to! {
        parser: GrammarParser,
        input: "abc",
        rule: Rule::string,
        tokens: [
            string(0, 3)
        ]
    };
}

#[test]
fn insensitive() {
    parses_to! {
        parser: GrammarParser,
        input: "aBC",
        rule: Rule::insensitive,
        tokens: [
            insensitive(0, 3)
        ]
    };
}

fn range() {
    parses_to! {
        parser: GrammarParser,
        input: "6",
        rule: Rule::range,
        tokens: [
            range(0, 1)
        ]
    };
}

full_range = { "a" | "b" | "c" }
charclass = { "a" | "b" | "c" | "x" | "y" | "z" }
partial = { "a" | ident | "b" | "c" | "d" }

cat > derive/tests/coalescing.rs <<'EOF'
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Licensed under MIT/Apache-2.0

extern crate pest;
#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_derive;

use pest::Parser;

#[derive(Parser)]
#[grammar = "tests/coalescing.pest"]
struct CoalescingParser;

#[test]
fn full_range() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::full_range, "a").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::full_range, "c").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::full_range, "d").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn charclass() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::charclass, "a").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::charclass, "y").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::charclass, "m").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn partial() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "a").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "c").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "foo").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "e").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn negated() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::negated, "z").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::negated, "a").is_err());
}
EOF

            OptimizedExpr::CharClass(vec![
                ("a".to_owned(), "b".to_owned()),
                ("x".to_owned(), "y".to_owned()),
            ]),
            str_("z"),
            range("m", "n"),
        ]);
        assert_eq!(coalesce_expr(original.clone()), original);
    }

    #[test]
    fn nested_choice_inside_seq_is_coalesced() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(choice(vec![str_("a"), str_("b"), str_("c")])),
            Box::new(str_("d")),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(original),
            OptimizedExpr::Seq(Box::new(range("a", "c")), Box::new(str_("d")))
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_restore_on_err_qualifies() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(choice(vec![
                OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(Box::new(str_("a"))),
                str_("b"),
                str_("c"),
            ])))),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ANY".to_owned())),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(original),
            OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())])
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_any_single_range() {"""

                    ("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned()),

+    /// Attempts to match a single character contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok` with
+    /// the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
+    /// otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
+    ///
+    /// # Examples
+    ///
+    /// ```
+    /// # use pest;
+    /// # #[allow(non_camel_case_types)]
+    /// # #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
+    /// enum Rule {}
+    ///
+    /// let input = "ab";
+    /// let mut state: Box<pest::ParserState<'_, Rule>> = pest::ParserState::new(input);
+    /// let mut result = state.match_char_class(&[('a', 'c')]);
+    /// assert!(result.is_ok());
+    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap().position().pos(), 1);
+    ///
+    /// state = pest::ParserState::new(input);
+    /// result = state.match_char_class(&[('x', 'z')]);
+    /// assert!(result.is_err());
+    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().position().pos(), 0);
+    /// ```
+    #[inline]
+    pub fn match_char_class(
+        mut self: Box<Self>,
+        ranges: &[(char, char)],
+    ) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
+        let start_position = self.position.pos();
+        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
+            ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
+        });
+        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
+            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
+            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
+        }
+        if succeeded {
+            Ok(self)
+        } else {
+            Err(self)
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Attempts to match a single character not contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok`
+    /// with the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated
+    /// `Box<ParserState>` otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
+    ///
+    /// # Examples
+    ///
+    /// ```
+    /// # use pest;
+    /// # #[allow(non_camel_case_types)]
+    /// # #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
+    /// enum Rule {}
+    ///
+    /// let input = "ab";
+    /// let mut state: Box<pest::ParserState<'_, Rule>> = pest::ParserState::new(input);
+    /// let mut result = state.match_neg_char_class(&[('b', 'z')]);
+    /// assert!(result.is_ok());
+    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap().position().pos(), 1);
+    ///
+    /// state = pest::ParserState::new(input);
+    /// result = state.match_neg_char_class(&[('a', 'c')]);
+    /// assert!(result.is_err());
+    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().position().pos(), 0);
+    /// ```
+    #[inline]
+    pub fn match_neg_char_class(
+        mut self: Box<Self>,
+        ranges: &[(char, char)],
+    ) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
+        let start_position = self.position.pos();
+        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
+            !ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
+        });
+        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
+            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
+            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
+        }
+        if succeeded {
+            Ok(self)
+        } else {
+            Err(self)
+        }
+    }
+

     /// if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>` otherwise.
     ///

path = 'meta/src/optimizer/coalescer.rs'
src = open(path).read()

old = """fn flatten_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> Vec<OptimizedExpr> {
    let mut alternatives = vec![lhs];
    let mut current = rhs;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) = current {
        alternatives.push(*l);
        current = *r;

}"""
new = """fn flatten_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> Vec<OptimizedExpr> {
    fn collect(expr: OptimizedExpr, out: &mut Vec<OptimizedExpr>) {
        match expr {
            OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) => {
                collect(*l, out);
                collect(*r, out);
            }
            expr => out.push(expr),
        }
    }

    let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
    collect(lhs, &mut alternatives);
    collect(rhs, &mut alternatives);
    alternatives
}"""

old = """fn collect_neg_ranges(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
    let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
    let mut current = expr;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) = current {
        alternatives.push(lhs.as_ref());
        current = rhs.as_ref();
    }
    alternatives.push(current);

    let mut ranges = Vec::new();
    for alt in alternatives {
        ranges.extend(ranges_of(alt)?);
    }
    Some(ranges)
}"""
new = """fn collect_neg_ranges(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
    fn collect<'a>(expr: &'a OptimizedExpr, out: &mut Vec<&'a OptimizedExpr>) {
        match expr {
            OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => {
                collect(lhs, out);
                collect(rhs, out);
            }
            expr => out.push(expr),
        }
    }

    let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
    collect(expr, &mut alternatives);

    let mut ranges = Vec::new();
    for alt in alternatives {
        ranges.extend(ranges_of(alt)?);
    }
    Some(ranges)
}"""
assert old in src

path = 'meta/src/optimizer/coalescer.rs'
src = open(path).read()

old = """fn coalesce_seq(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    if let OptimizedExpr::NegPred(expr) = &lhs {
        if let OptimizedExpr::Ident(name) = &rhs {
            if name == "ANY" {
                if let Some(ranges) = collect_neg_ranges(expr) {
                    let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
                    if !merged.is_empty() {
                        return OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(to_string_ranges(merged));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    OptimizedExpr::Seq(Box::new(coalesce_expr(lhs)), Box::new(coalesce_expr(rhs)))
}"""
new = """fn coalesce_seq(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    let mut elements = vec![lhs];
    let mut current = rhs;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Seq(l, r) = current {
        elements.push(*l);
        current = *r;
    }
    elements.push(current);

    let mut result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < elements.len() {
        if i + 1 < elements.len() {
            if let OptimizedExpr::NegPred(expr) = &elements[i] {
                if let OptimizedExpr::Ident(name) = &elements[i + 1] {
                    if name == "ANY" {
                        if let Some(ranges) = collect_neg_ranges(expr) {
                            let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
                            if !merged.is_empty() {
                                result.push(OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(to_string_ranges(merged)));
                                i += 2;
                                continue;
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        result.push(elements[i].clone());
        i += 1;
    }

    let result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = result.into_iter().map(coalesce_expr).collect();
    rebuild_seq(result)
}"""

     8	// modified, or distributed except according to those terms.
     9	
    10	use crate::optimizer::*;
    11	
    12	pub fn coalesce(rule: OptimizedRule) -> OptimizedRule {
    13	    let OptimizedRule { name, ty, expr } = rule;
    14	    OptimizedRule {
    15	        name,
    16	        ty,
    17	        expr: coalesce_expr(expr),
    18	    }
    19	}
    20	
    21	fn coalesce_expr(expr: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    22	    match expr {
    23	        OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_choice(*lhs, *rhs),
    24	        OptimizedExpr::Seq(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_seq(*lhs, *rhs),
    25	        OptimizedExpr::PosPred(expr) => OptimizedExpr::PosPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
    26	        OptimizedExpr::NegPred(expr) => OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
    27	        OptimizedExpr::Rep(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Rep(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
    28	        OptimizedExpr::Opt(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Opt(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
    29	        OptimizedExpr::Push(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Push(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
    30	        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
    31	        OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(expr) => OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
    32	        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
    33	        OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(expr, tag) => {
    34	            OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr)), tag)
    35	        }
    36	        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(expr) => {
    37	            OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr)))
    38	        }
    39	        expr => expr,
    40	    }
    41	}
    42	
    43	fn coalesce_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    44	    let alternatives = flatten_choice(lhs, rhs);
    45	    let qualified: Vec<Option<Vec<(char, char)>>> = alternatives.iter().map(ranges_of).collect();
    46	
    47	    let mut result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    48	    let mut i = 0;
    49	    while i < alternatives.len() {
    50	        if qualified[i].is_some() {
    51	            let mut j = i;
    52	            while j < alternatives.len() && qualified[j].is_some() {
    53	                j += 1;
    54	            }
    55	
    56	            let run_len = j - i;
    57	            let all_qualified = i == 0 && j == alternatives.len();
    58	            let ranges: Vec<(char, char)> = (i..j)
    59	                .flat_map(|k| qualified[k].as_ref().expect("qualified").iter().copied())
    60	                .collect();
    61	            let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
    62	
    63	            let coalesce = if all_qualified {
    64	                merged.len() < run_len
    65	            } else {
    66	                run_len >= 3 && merged.len() < run_len
    67	            };
    68	
    69	            if coalesce {
    70	                match build_char_expr(merged) {
    71	                    Some(expr) => result.push(expr),
    72	                    None => result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned()),
    73	                }
    74	            } else {
    75	                result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned());
    76	            }
    77	
    78	            i = j;
    79	        } else {
    80	            result.push(alternatives[i].clone());
    81	            i += 1;
    82	        }
    83	    }
    84	
    85	    let result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = result.into_iter().map(coalesce_expr).collect();
    86	    rebuild_choice(result)
    87	}
    88	
    89	fn coalesce_seq(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    90	    let mut elements = vec![lhs];
    91	    let mut current = rhs;
    92	    while let OptimizedExpr::Seq(l, r) = current {
    93	        elements.push(*l);
    94	        current = *r;
    95	    }
    96	    elements.push(current);
    97	
    98	    let mut result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    99	    let mut i = 0;
   100	    while i < elements.len() {
   101	        if i + 1 < elements.len() {
   102	            if let OptimizedExpr::NegPred(expr) = &elements[i] {
   103	                if let OptimizedExpr::Ident(name) = &elements[i + 1] {
   104	                    if name == "ANY" {
   105	                        if let Some(ranges) = collect_neg_ranges(expr) {
   106	                            let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
   107	                            if !merged.is_empty() {
   108	                                result.push(OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(to_string_ranges(merged)));
   109	                                i
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
             continue;
   111	                            }
   112	                        }
   113	                    }
   114	                }
   115	            }
   116	        }
   117	        result.push(elements[i].clone());
   118	        i += 1;
   119	    }
   120	
   121	    let result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = result.into_iter().map(coalesce_expr).collect();
   122	    rebuild_seq(result)
   123	}
   124	
   125	fn flatten_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> Vec<OptimizedExpr> {
   126	    fn collect(expr: OptimizedExpr, out: &mut Vec<OptimizedExpr>) {
   127	        match expr {
   128	            OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) => {
   129	                collect(*l, out);
   130	                collect(*r, out);
   131	            }
   132	            expr => out.push(expr),
   133	        }
   134	    }
   135	
   136	    let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
   137	    collect(lhs, &mut alternatives);
   138	    collect(rhs, &mut alternatives);
   139	    alternatives
   140	}
   141	
   142	fn rebuild_choice(alternatives: Vec<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
   143	    let mut iter = alternatives.into_iter().rev();
   144	    let mut acc = iter.next().expect("non-empty choice");
   145	    for alt in iter {
   146	        acc = OptimizedExpr::Choice(Box::new(alt), Box::new(acc));
   147	    }
   148	    acc
   149	}
   150	
   151	fn rebuild_seq(elements: Vec<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
   152	    let mut iter = elements.into_iter().rev();
   153	    let mut acc = iter.next().expect("non-empty sequence");
   154	    for elem in iter {
   155	        acc = OptimizedExpr::Seq(Box::new(elem), Box::new(acc));
   156	    }
   157	    acc
   158	}
   159	
   160	fn collect_neg_ranges(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
   161	    fn collect<'a>(expr: &'a OptimizedExpr, out: &mut Vec<&'a OptimizedExpr>) {
   162	        match expr {
   163	            OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => {
   164	                collect(lhs, out);
   165	                collect(rhs, out);
   166	            }
   167	            expr => out.push(expr),
   168	        }
   169	    }
   170	
   171	    let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
   172	    collect(expr, &mut alternatives);
   173	
   174	    let mut ranges = Vec::new();
   175	    for alt in alternatives {
   176	        ranges.extend(ranges_of(alt)?);
   177	    }
   178	    Some(ranges)
   179	}
   180	
   181	fn ranges_of(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
   182	    match expr {
   183	        OptimizedExpr::Str(string) if string.chars().count() == 1 => {
   184	            let c = string.chars().next().expect("single char");
   185	            Some(vec![(c, c)])
   186	        }
   187	        OptimizedExpr::Insens(string) if string.chars().count() == 1 => {
   188	            let c = string.chars().next().expect("single char");
   189	            if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() {
   190	                Some(vec![
   191	                    (c.to_ascii_lowercase(), c.to_ascii_lowercase()),
   192	                    (c.to_ascii_uppercase(), c.to_ascii_uppercase()),
   193	                ])
   194	            } else {
   195	                Some(vec![(c, c)])
   196	            }
   197	        }
   198	        OptimizedExpr::Range(start, end)
   199	            if start.chars().count() == 1 && end.chars().count() == 1 =>
   200	        {
   201	            Some(vec![(
   202	                start.chars().next().expect("single char"),
   203	                end.chars().next().expect("single char"),
   204	            )])
   205	        }
   206	        OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ranges) => {
   207	            if ranges.is_empty() {
   208	                return None;
   209	            }
   210	            let mut result = Vec::with_capacity(ranges.len());
   211	            for (start, end) in ranges {
   212	                if start.chars().count() != 1 || end.chars().count() != 1 {
   213	                    return None;
   214	                }
   215	                result.push((
   216	                    start.chars().next().expect("single char"),
   217	                    end.chars().next().expect("single char"),
   218	                ));
   219	            }
   220	            Some(result)
   221	        }
   222	        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(expr) => ranges_of(expr),
   223	        _ => None,
   224	    }
   225	}
   226	
   227	fn merge_ranges(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Vec<(char, char)> {
   228	    let mut ranges = ranges;
   229	    ranges.sort_by_key(|(start, _)| *start as u32);
   230	
   231	    let mut merged: Vec<(char, char)> = Vec::new();
   232	    for (start, end) in ranges {
   233	        if let Some((_, last_end)) = merged.last_mut() {
   234	            if *last_end as u32 + 1 >= start as u32 {
   235	                if end > *last_end {
   236	                    *last_end = end;
   237	                }
   238	                continue;
   239	            }
   240	        }

[ARCHIVE ELIDED 33 CHARS]

   372	fn generate_skip(rules: &[OptimizedRule]) -> TokenStream {
   373	    let whitespace = rules.iter().any(|rule| rule.name == "WHITESPACE");
   374	    let comment = rules.iter().any(|rule| rule.name == "COMMENT");
   375	
   376	    match (whitespace, comment) {
   377	        (false, false) => generate_rule!(skip, Ok(state)),
   378	        (true, false) => generate_rule!(
   379	            skip,
   380	            if state.atomicity() == ::pest::Atomicity::NonAtomic {
   381	                state.repeat(|state| super::visible::WHITESPACE(state))
   382	            } else {
   383	                Ok(state)
   384	            }
   385	        ),
   386	        (false, true) => generate_rule!(
   387	            skip,
   388	            if state.atomicity() == ::pest::Atomicity::NonAtomic {
   389	                state.repeat(|state| super::visible::COMMENT(state))
   390	            } else {
   391	                Ok(state)

   393	        ),

   405	                                    })
   406	                                })

import sys, json
lines = sys.stdin.read().splitlines()
def parse(s):
    # s is like '  left: "..."'
    _, _, raw = s.partition(': ')
    # raw starts and ends with double quote; Rust Debug escapes. Use codecs decode of Rust escapes roughly.
    # We'll just show repr and count backslashes.
    return raw
for l in lines:
    raw = l.split(': ',1)[1]
    # count backslashes in raw
    print(l[:8], "len", len(raw), "bs", raw.count('\\'))
    # show the token part
    idx = raw.find('tokens:')
    print("  ", raw[idx:idx+120])

   103	/// Function mapping rule to its helper message defined by user.
   104	pub type RuleToMessageFn<R> = Box<dyn Fn(&R) -> Option<String>>;
   105	/// Function mapping string element to bool denoting whether it's a whitespace defined by user.
   106	pub type IsWhitespaceFn = Box<dyn Fn(String) -> bool>;
   107	
   108	impl ParsingToken {
   109	    pub fn is_whitespace(&self, is_whitespace: &IsWhitespaceFn) -> bool {
   110	        match self {
   111	            ParsingToken::Sensitive { token } => is_whitespace(token.clone()),
   112	            ParsingToken::Insensitive { token } => is_whitespace(token.clone()),
   113	            ParsingToken::Range { .. } => false,
   114	            ParsingToken::BuiltInRule => false,
   115	        }
   116	    }
   117	}

path = 'pest/src/parser_state.rs'

# 1. Add enum variants
old = """#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub enum ParsingToken {
    Sensitive { token: String },
    Insensitive { token: String },
    Range { start: char, end: char },
    BuiltInRule,
}"""
new = """#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub enum ParsingToken {
    Sensitive { token: String },
    Insensitive { token: String },
    Range { start: char, end: char },
    CharClass { ranges: Vec<(char, char)> },
    NegCharClass { ranges: Vec<(char, char)> },
    BuiltInRule,
}"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# 2. Update Display
old = """            ParsingToken::Range { start, end } => write!(f, "{start}..{end}"),
            ParsingToken::BuiltInRule => write!(f, "BUILTIN_RULE"),"""
new = """            ParsingToken::Range { start, end } => write!(f, "{start}..{end}"),
            ParsingToken::CharClass { ranges } => {
                let ranges = ranges
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(start, end)| format!("{:?}..{:?}", start, end))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(" | ");
                write!(f, "{ranges}")
            }
            ParsingToken::NegCharClass { ranges } => {
                let ranges = ranges
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(start, end)| format!("{:?}..{:?}", start, end))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(" | ");
                write!(f, "{ranges}")
            }
            ParsingToken::BuiltInRule => write!(f, "BUILTIN_RULE"),"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# 3. Update match_char_class to use CharClass token
old = """        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            for (start, end) in ranges {
                let token = ParsingToken::Range {
                    start: *start,
                    end: *end,
                };
                self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
            }
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to match a single character not contained in any of the given ranges."""
new = """        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::CharClass {
                ranges: ranges.to_vec(),
            };
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to match a single character not contained in any of the given ranges."""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# 4. Update match_neg_char_class to use NegCharClass token
old = """        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to skip `n` characters forward."""
new = """        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::NegCharClass {
                ranges: ranges.to_vec(),
            };
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to skip `n` characters forward."""

   209	
   210	impl Display for ParsingToken {
   211	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> core::fmt::Result {
   212	        match self {
   213	            ParsingToken::Sensitive { token } => write!(f, "{token}"),
   214	            ParsingToken::Insensitive { token } => write!(f, "{}", token.to_uppercase()),
   215	            ParsingToken::Range { start, end } => write!(f, "{start}..{end}"),
   216	            ParsingToken::CharClass { ranges } => {
   217	                let ranges = ranges
   218	                    .iter()
   219	                    .map(|(start, end)| format!("{:?}..{:?}", start, end))
   220	                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
   221	                    .join(" | ");
   222	                write!(f, "{ranges}")
   223	            }
   224	            ParsingToken::NegCharClass { ranges } => {
   225	                let ranges = ranges
   226	                    .iter()
   227	                    .map(|(start, end)| format!("{:?}..{:?}", start, end))
   228	                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
   229	                    .join(" | ");
   230	                write!(f, "{ranges}")
   231	            }
   232	            ParsingToken::BuiltInRule => write!(f, "BUILTIN_RULE"),
   233	        }
   234	    }
   235	}
   236	/// A helper that provides efficient string handling without unnecessary copying.
   237	/// We use `Arc<String>` instead of `Cow<'i, str>` to avoid copying strings when cloning the `Owned` variant, since
   238	/// `Arc::clone` only increments a reference count. [SpanOrLiteral] needs to be [Send] and [Sync]`, so we use [Arc]
   239	/// instead of [Rc].
   240	///

END STEP

old = """            ParsingToken::CharClass { ranges } => {
                let ranges = ranges
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(start, end)| format!("{:?}..{:?}", start, end))

COMMAND:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
path = 'grammars/src/lib.rs'
src = open(path, encoding='utf-8').read()

start_marker = 'let table_creation_without_primary_key ='
end_marker = 'let user_creation_password_without_single_quotes ='

start = src.find(start_marker)
end = src.find(end_marker)
assert start != -1 and end != -1 and start < end

new_block = '''        let table_creation_without_primary_key =
            r#"create table t(col_1 int,) distributed by (col_1)"#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(table_creation_without_primary_key),
            [
                " --> 1:26",
                "  |",
                "1 | create table t(col_1 int,) distributed by (col_1)",
                "  |                          ^---",
                "  |",
                "  = error: parsing error occurred.",
                r#"    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `"`, `'-'..'-' | 'A'..'Z' | '_'..'_' | 'a'..'z' | 'А'..'я'`, `'\t'..'\n' | ' '..' '`, `PRIMARY`"#,
                "    help: Expected table creation.",
                "          - Add primary key consisting of non nullable table columns.",
            ]
            .join("\\n")
        );

'''
# In the raw string in new_block, '\t' should be literal backslash-t.
# Python source above: within r#"..."#, \t is literal backslash+t? No, this is Python triple-quoted,
# so \t IS a tab. We need to write \\t to get backslash+t.
# Let's fix the raw string line by replacing the raw string content.
# Actually let's build the note line separately with explicit backslashes.
new_block = new_block.replace("`'\\t'..'\\n'`", "`'\\\\t'..'\\\\n'`")

src = src[:start] + new_block + src[end:]
open(path, 'w', encoding='utf-8').write(src)
print("replaced block")

COMMAND:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
path = 'grammars/src/lib.rs'
src = open(path, encoding='utf-8').read()

start_marker = 'let table_creation_without_primary_key ='
end_marker = 'let user_creation_password_without_single_quotes ='
start = src.find(start_marker)
end = src.find(end_marker)
assert start != -1 and end != -1

note_line = 'r#"' + r"    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `\"`, `'-'..'-' | 'A'..'Z' | '_'..'_' | 'a'..'z' | 'А'..'я'`, `'\t'..'\n' | ' '..' '`, `PRIMARY`" + '"#,'
# r"..." in Python keeps \t as backslash+t.

new_block = '''        let table_creation_without_primary_key =
            r#"create table t(col_1 int,) distributed by (col_1)"#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(table_creation_without_primary_key),

                " --> 1:26",

                __NOTE_LINE__,
                "    help: Expected table creation.",
                "          - Add primary key consisting of non nullable table columns.",
            ]
            .join("\\n")
        );

+    /// Attempts to match a single character contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok` with
+    /// the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
+    /// otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
+    ///
+    /// # Examples
+    ///
+    /// ```
+    /// # use pest;
+    /// # #[allow(non_camel_case_types)]
+    /// # #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
+    /// enum Rule {}
+    ///
+    /// let input = "ab";
+    /// let mut state: Box<pest::ParserState<'_, Rule>> = pest::ParserState::new(input);
+    /// let mut result = state.match_char_class(&[('a', 'c')]);
+    /// assert!(result.is_ok());
+    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap().position().pos(), 1);
+    ///
+    /// state = pest::ParserState::new(input);
+    /// result = state.match_char_class(&[('x', 'z')]);
+    /// assert!(result.is_err());
+    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().position().pos(), 0);
+    /// ```
+    #[inline]
+    pub fn match_char_class(
+        mut self: Box<Self>,
+        ranges: &[(char, char)],
+    ) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
+        let start_position = self.position.pos();
+        let succeeded = self
+            .position
+            .match_char_by(|c| ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end));
+        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
+            let token = ParsingToken::CharClass {
+                ranges: ranges.to_vec(),
+            };
+            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
+        }
+        if succeeded {
+            Ok(self)
+        } else {
+            Err(self)
+        }
+    }
+

+    #[inline]
+    pub fn match_neg_char_class(

--- derive/tests/coalescing.rs ---
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Licensed under MIT/Apache-2.0

extern crate pest;
#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_derive;

use pest::Parser;

#[derive(Parser)]
#[grammar = "tests/coalescing.pest"]
struct CoalescingParser;

#[test]
fn full_range() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::full_range, "a").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::full_range, "c").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::full_range, "d").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn charclass() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::charclass, "a").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::charclass, "y").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::charclass, "m").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn partial() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "a").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "c").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "foo").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::partial, "e").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn negated() {
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::negated, "z").is_ok());
    assert!(CoalescingParser::parse(Rule::negated, "a").is_err());
}
--- meta/src/optimizer/coalescer.rs ---
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Copyright (c) 2018 Dragoș Tiselice
//
// Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0
// <LICENSE-APACHE or http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0> or the MIT
// license <LICENSE-MIT or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT>, at your
// option. All files in the project carrying such notice may not be copied,
// modified, or distributed except according to those terms.

use crate::optimizer::*;

pub fn coalesce(rule: OptimizedRule) -> OptimizedRule {
    let OptimizedRule { name, ty, expr } = rule;
    OptimizedRule {
        name,
        ty,
        expr: coalesce_expr(expr),
    }
}

fn coalesce_expr(expr: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    match expr {
        OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_choice(*lhs, *rhs),
        OptimizedExpr::Seq(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_seq(*lhs, *rhs),
        OptimizedExpr::PosPred(expr) => OptimizedExpr::PosPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::NegPred(expr) => OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::Rep(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Rep(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::Opt(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Opt(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::Push(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Push(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(expr) => OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(expr, tag) => {
            OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr)), tag)
        }
        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(expr) => {
            OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr)))
        }
        expr => expr,
    }
}

fn coalesce_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    let alternatives = flatten_choice(lhs, rhs);
    let qualified: Vec<Option<Vec<(char, char)>>> = alternatives.iter().map(ranges_of).collect();

    let mut result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < alternatives.len() {
        if qualified[i].is_some() {
            let mut j = i;
            while j < alternatives.len() && qualified[j].is_some() {
                j += 1;
            }

            let run_len = j - i;
            let all_qualified = i == 0 && j == alternatives.len();
            let ranges: Vec<(char, char)> = (i..j)
                .flat_map(|k| qualified[k].as_ref().expect("qualified").iter().copied())
                .collect();
            let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);

            let coalesce = if all_qualified {
                merged.len() < run_len
            } else {
                run_len >= 3 && merged.len() < run_len
            };

            if coalesce {
                match build_char_expr(merged) {

[OUTPUT_TAIL]
      );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_any_inside_longer_seq() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(choice(vec![
                str_("a"),
                str_("b"),
            ])))),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Seq(
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ANY".to_owned())),
                Box::new(str_("z")),
            )),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(original),
            OptimizedExpr::Seq(
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![(
                    "a".to_owned(),
                    "b".to_owned(),
                )])),
                Box::new(str_("z")),
            )
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_any_single_range() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(range("a", "z")))),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ANY".to_owned())),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(original),
            OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("a".to_owned(), "z".to_owned())])
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_partial_not_collapsed() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(choice(vec![
                str_("a"),
                OptimizedExpr::Ident("x".to_owned()),
            ])))),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ANY".to_owned())),
        );
        assert_eq!(coalesce_expr(original.clone()), original);
    }
}
--- vm/tests/coalescing.pest ---
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Licensed under MIT/Apache-2.0

full_range = { "a" | "b" | "c" }
two_ranges = { "a" | "b" | "c" | "x" | "y" | "z" }
partial = { "a" | ident | "b" | "c" | "d" }
ident = { "foo" }
negated = { !("a" | "b" | "c") ~ ANY }
negated_single = { !"x" ~ ANY }
mixed = { ^"a" | "b" | "c" | "d" }
--- vm/tests/coalescing.rs ---
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Licensed under MIT/Apache-2.0

extern crate pest;
extern crate pest_meta;
extern crate pest_vm;

use pest_meta::optimizer::{OptimizedExpr, OptimizedRule};
use pest_meta::parser::Rule;
use pest_meta::{optimizer, parser};
use pest_vm::Vm;

const GRAMMAR: &str = include_str!("coalescing.pest");

fn optimized() -> Vec<OptimizedRule> {
    let pairs = parser::parse(Rule::grammar_rules, GRAMMAR).unwrap();
    let ast = parser::consume_rules(pairs).unwrap();
    optimizer::optimize(ast)
}

#[test]
fn full_range_collapses() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "full_range").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::Range("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())
    );
}

#[test]
fn char_class_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "two_ranges").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::CharClass(vec![
            ("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned()),
            ("x".to_owned(), "z".to_owned()),
        ])
    );
}

#[test]
fn partial_run_is_coalesced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "partial").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::Choice(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Str("a".to_owned())),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Choice(
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ident".to_owned())),
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Range("b".to_owned(), "d".to_owned())),
            )),
        )
    );
}

#[test]
fn neg_char_class_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "negated").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())])
    );
}

#[test]
fn neg_char_class_single_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "negated_single").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("x".to_owned(), "x".to_owned())])
    );
}

#[test]
fn vm_parses_char_class() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let vm = Vm::new(rules);
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "c").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "d").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "b").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "y").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "m").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "c").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "foo").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "e").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("negated", "d").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated", "a").is_err());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated_single", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated_single", "x").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "A").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "d").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "e").is_err());
}

STEP 192 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && git commit -m "feat: add CharClass and NegCharClass coalescing optimization

Add CharClass(Vec<(String, String)>) and NegCharClass(Vec<(String, String)>)
variants to OptimizedExpr and a final coalescing optimizer pass.

Choice chains of qualifying single-character Str, Insens, Range, and
CharClass alternatives collapse into merged, sorted character ranges.
Case-insensitive alphabetic characters expand to both letter cases, and
overlapping/adjacent ranges are merged. RestoreOnErr wrappers are
stripped from qualifying alternatives. A result is emitted only when it
reduces the number of ranges, and a single merged range simplifies to a
Range or Str.

A negated predicate over qualifying alternatives followed by ANY
collapses into NegCharClass with the merged excluded ranges.

The generator, VM, and ParserState gain support for matching char
classes, including dedicated ParsingToken variants for error reporting." && git log --oneline -3 && git status --short
